### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T03:09:33.165Z

You're right: I had your approved screen and kept changing it. I've stopped. This version doesn't touch your artwork at all.

**What's in the video (9.6 seconds of real gameplay):**
- The scenery is your approved Screen 05 picture, the same painted version you called beautiful. The tunnel, gold bands, purple machinery and the pink-and-blue S-river are untouched, and the water still flows.
- The pink character is the real 3D Benny. He paddles, leans and steers left and right while he swims up your painted S-river. He passes through the gold bands into the tunnel, and Screen 06 opens from there.
- He starts in the same spot and at the same size as the Benny in your picture, then gets smaller as he swims deeper into the tunnel.
- Coins sit on the river and he swims into them. The counter went from 1,420 to 1,510 during the run and he lost no hearts.

**One visible flaw, honestly:** your picture had Benny painted in the foreground. When he swims away, the water where he was painted shows through as a slightly purple stripe down the middle of the river. It's in the comparison picture. Across every frame of Screen 05, the run measures 74% pixel likeness and 80% "looks the same" to your approved picture. The painted version with Benny parked in his painted spot measured 82% and 93%. The gap comes from Benny leaving his painted spot and that stripe, not from any change to the scenery.

**Your direct question: can the current approach do what you asked?** Partly. Your painting shows the tunnel from one fixed viewpoint. Every time the camera flew forward with Benny, everything around him had to be rebuilt as 3D models, and that rebuilt world never looked like your painting. That's why every 3D version drifted from your artwork. This version keeps your picture still and moves Benny through it, the way games with painted backgrounds work. If you want the camera itself to fly through the tunnel and still look like your painting, that needs a dedicated 3D environment artist to build the tunnel properly. More rounds of tweaks from me won't get there.

**Nothing lost, no credits spent:**
- **New version:** `?paint=1&paintswim=1&benny31=1`
- **Painted version you liked:** `?paint=1`, identical to before.
- **Rejected 3D version:** kept as it was.
- The default game is unchanged.
- **Backup:** everything is on GitHub, branch `claude/screen-05-3d-geometry-9yu7pu`, commit `4ff6a8d`, remote matches, and the five-minute autosave is running. At startup the session couldn't download from GitHub, so I haven't checked for newer work on other branches.

Is this what you wanted: your exact screen with Benny really swimming through it? If yes, I'll fix the purple stripe next and change nothing else. I won't touch other screens until you say so.

- The scenery is your approved picture itself, not rebuilt artwork. The gold rings, purple machinery, paddles, foam and river come toward him and pass out of the frame, with new rings coming from the tunnel end. Your painted water keeps flowing.
- Coins come down the river at him and pop as he collects them: 1,330 → 1,420. He lost no hearts.
- The run goes from the end of Screen 04, all through Screen 05, into Screen 06. In Screen 06 he stays the real 3D Benny, never a painted picture.

**How it works:** each gold ring in your painting is about 1.7 times bigger than the next one, all around the tunnel end. So moving forward one ring means enlarging your picture by that amount, and the loop then returns to your picture exactly. That's why every frame still looks like your artwork.

**Flaws you'll see:**
- **Brief doubling:** each time a ring passes, the river and the bottom gold frame look doubled for a split second while the loop closes.
- **Screen 05 → 06 handover:** for about half a second the view goes blurry and doubled.
- **Straight flight:** Benny flies straight down the tunnel. The view doesn't swing around each S-bend, though the bends themselves pass under him.
- **Purple patch:** behind Benny there's still the slightly purple patch where he was painted.
- **Coins:** a coin sometimes overlaps his hat for an instant just before he collects it.
- **Likeness score:** a pixel-by-pixel score against your still picture no longer means much, because the whole view is moving. It drops to about 8%. Judge it by eye.

### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T03:33:14.387Z

**No.** With this project and these tools, I can't give you real 3D swimming that stays faithful to your approved pictures from start to end with no visible differences. Here are the straight answers.

**1. Can I achieve both?** No. Your approved screen is one painting seen from one spot. Real 3D swimming needs a whole tunnel that can be seen from every position along the way. Everything your painting doesn't show (the sides, what's ahead, what's behind) has to be built or generated, and that part never matches the painting exactly. Every attempt in this project shows the same thing. The still painted screen measured about 94% like your picture. Every version where the camera really moves through 3D measured about 5–12%.

**2. Is there a technical solution different from what failed?** There is one thing not yet tried properly, and I can't promise it works. It uses World Labs Atlas, which is already connected. Atlas would generate many views travelling down your tunnel from your picture, then combine them into a photographic-style 3D scene. Benny would swim through it with a real follow-camera, with real flowing water and coins added on top. In November the project tried a simpler version, a 3D world made from your single picture. The far third of the tunnel and the S-curved river collapsed, so it wasn't usable. The multi-view method is the step up from that. Otherwise the industry answer is a dedicated 3D environment artist who builds the tunnel by hand in Blender from your painting.

**3. What differences would remain, even if it works?**
- Details further down the tunnel would be invented, not yours.
- Close-up areas could look soft or blurry.
- The glow and lighting would be flatter than the painting.
- The water would be animated, not your exact painted streaks.
- The S-curves may not follow your painting exactly.
- It might have to be simplified to run smoothly on cheaper Android phones.

**4. Can I demonstrate it on one short section of Screen 05 first?** Yes, but it costs World Labs credits. Before spending anything I would give you the exact job and credit cost and wait for your OK. I won't touch other screens unless it works on that section.

**5. What can't I deliver?** "Exactly my approved screen in every frame of genuine 3D gameplay" is beyond what I can build. A 3D environment artist would come closer than I can, but even then it would match your art in style, not pixel for pixel. Anything better than "clearly your screen, with visible differences" would be a guess, not a promise.

Everything you have is saved and unchanged:
- the painted version, `?paint=1`
- both Benny-swimming tests
- the rejected 3D version

Do you want me to quote the credit cost for the one-section Screen 05 test? If not, I'll stop here.

### 🧑 User — 2026-11-21T03:34:47.463Z

Base directory for this skill: /root/.claude/plugins/cache/worldlabs/worldlabs/0.2.2/skills/atlas-splats

# Atlas splats

`posedImages2Splats` reconstructs a scene from posed views with depth, then refines it into an
SPZ file. It is the final step, and one run takes 15 to 25 minutes.
While refining it generates its own dense views, which it does not return.

What you control is the set of views you give it, so a good result comes from getting that set
right. The views are also the preview of the result.

This file and [references/scene.md](references/scene.md) are the method, and the request shapes
are in `assets/example/`. Read both files in full before starting. The Atlas docs are reference
for a task's fields and limits: open a task's page when a request needs a field these files do
not show, or when one is rejected. The docs also have shorter workflow pages written for people;
where they differ from these files, follow these files.

## Steps

1. **Agree where to work, then build the scene.** Before anything else, check whether Blender
   is installed and ask the person where to work. With Blender installed, the choice is:

   - **Open Blender**, so they can watch the scene build and scrub through it; or
   - **Build in the background** and hand over the `.blend` at the end.

   If they choose the open window, open it as soon as the first image is posed, before any
   views or path, so they watch from the start. Without Blender, work in the shell.
   [references/scene.md](references/scene.md) has the question for each setup and the steps:
   pose the images, build the scene out in rounds of a few views until it is blocked out with no
   gaps, and show it.

2. **Check the set as reconstruction input.** The posed frames and the views you kept are the
   input. A good set:

   - shows every area the person will look at: the floor, low furniture, walls, and the far side
     of anything they will move around. Areas no view shows come out blurry or invented;
   - agrees with itself. An object with a different shape or place in two views becomes a
     doubled, ghosted object;
   - has depth over most of every frame, and one size for all frames. A view can look right and
     still have depth on only a small part of it.

   For a single room, 12 to 16 views in all is a good range. To add coverage, send another
   `atlasGenerate` request with the current views as `contextFrames` and `"returnDepth": true`.

3. **Reconstruct once.** Submit it as soon as the set is good, and tell the person it is
   running and how long it takes. The request lists each frame's image, depth and camera; see
   [the example request](assets/example/splats-request.json).

   ```sh
   worldlabs tasks run posedImages2Splats --request splats.json --json > splats_op.json
   worldlabs operations wait <operation-id> --timeout 3600 --json
   ```

   Submit without `--wait` and keep the operation ID. If the wait times out, wait again. A
   second submission while the first runs starts a second full run.

4. **Hand it over.**

   ```sh
   worldlabs operations download <operation-id> --out splats
   ```

   The result is an SPZ file named for its splat count (`refinedSplats_2000000.spz`), in the
   same world as the cameras you sent. Look at it before you report: from the start view, and
   from places the person will go.

   Give the person the file, a way to look at it, the operation ID, and which parts of the scene
   the model invented. In Blender the splats import into the scene they have been watching.
   Otherwise see [Rendering with Spark](https://docs.atlas.worldlabs.ai/rendering-with-spark.md).

## Example

[A complete run](references/example.md): one photo of a potter's studio, the room built out in
three rounds of views, and one reconstruction from the ten frames, with pictures of the result.
Its requests and results are in `assets/example/`. It shows one scene's choices, not values to
copy.

## Facts

- Bounded, textured interiors reconstruct best. Sky, foliage, thin structures, glass, faces and
  large plain walls are where artifacts appear, and so are surfaces that only one view sees at
  close range. Say so when the scene has them.
- `prompt` describes the scene. Leave it out and Atlas writes one from the images.
- For a mesh, pass the SPZ asset to `splats2Mesh` as `splatAsset`.
- A video of the same scene uses the same views; see the `atlas-flythrough` skill.
- An HTTP 422 means the request was rejected. The CLI does not print the reason; check that
  every frame has `imageAsset`, `depth.depthAsset` and `camera`, and that all cameras share one
  size.
- CLI setup, errors and other tasks are in the `worldlabs-cli` skill. Guides:
  https://docs.atlas.worldlabs.ai/posed-images-to-splats.md and
  https://docs.atlas.worldlabs.ai/splat-best-practices.md

>   each file's local path. If the command is missing, update the CLI.
> - **Errors.** The CLI does not print the API's reason. HTTP 422 means the request body was
>   rejected: check it against the task's guide page. HTTP 402 means the project is out of credits
>   or over its spend limit. HTTP 401 or 403 is the key or its permissions. An operation that ends
>   `THROTTLED` hit a capacity limit, not a problem with the request: tell the person, wait a few
>   minutes, and run it again. See https://docs.atlas.worldlabs.ai/errors.md
> 
> Tasks spend the project's credits. Run the tasks the request needs without quoting prices or

> ### World generation pricing
> Generating a world using the [World Generation API](https://docs.worldlabs.ai/api/reference/worlds/generate.md) is the most common API request. However, the number of usage events and the cost of generating a world depends on the input type.
> The World Generation API requires a panorama image (pano) to convert into a 3D world, so it first generates a pano from your input if a pano is not provided. ### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T04:52:55.447Z

The Marble test worked for a short stretch: the camera follows Benny forward through a real 3D world that still looks like your Screen 05. It only holds for about 2.4 seconds of swimming. Your Atlas credits weren't needed; this used only your existing Marble credits.

**Credits:** 230 for the draft plus the 1,580 you approved, so your balance went from 5,420 to 3,610. Nothing was bought, no Atlas job was started, and no card can be charged because every job stayed far below your balance.

**What works:**
- **Real 3D:** Marble built a 3D tunnel from your full approved Screen 05, with Benny, coins and the score bar removed first. The views from four other angles show it, including one from outside: a 3D tube with gold rings and the coins inside.
- **Gameplay:** the improved 3D Benny paddles and steers along the S-path from your painting. The camera starts exactly at your picture's view and follows behind him. The bottom gold frame passes behind him, rings and purple machinery surround him, and coins go 1,420 → 1,520. Normal speed, no cuts.
- **Closeness:** it looks like your Screen 05 throughout that stretch.

**Where it falls short:**
- **Too short:** one Marble world holds up for only about 2.4 seconds of normal-speed swimming, then it smears and breaks up (the forward-travel sheet shows where). Covering all of Screen 05 (about 7 seconds) would need two or three more worlds, each started from the end of the previous one. Those can only be made from views rendered out of this world, not from your art, so each one would likely drift further from your painting. Your 3,610 credits cover two more.
- **Look:** softer than your painting, with less glow and fewer bubbles.
- **Water:** frozen. The river doesn't flow yet; that needs a separate animated water layer.
- **Benny:** his front and sides are plainer than his back.
- **Coins:** up close they show a faint square edge.

**Android:** the world uses 500,000 splats, within the 1–2 million the renderer's makers recommend for Android phones. But a tunnel seen from inside is the demanding case, and I can't measure a real phone here. Putting it into the game also means upgrading the 3D engine (three.js 0.170 → 0.180), which I haven't done. Only a test build on your phone gives a real answer.

This was a separate test viewer; your game, approved artwork and all earlier versions are unchanged. Everything is saved on GitHub, branch `claude/screen-05-3d-geometry-9yu7pu`, commit `af01076`.

**Two options, nothing started without your OK:**
1. **Phone test (free):** put this one world into the game and build an Android test version, so you can feel it on your phone.
2. **Longer section (1,580 credits per world):** spend another 1,580 on a second world to lengthen the section, knowing it will likely look less like your art than the first one.

### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T09:57:06.493Z

Yes, there's a way to do it, and it costs no credits. Real mobile runner games build long levels from repeating pieces. I'd take the sharpest stretch of the 3D world you already paid for, one section of tunnel between two gold rings. Then I repeat it end to end for the whole of Screen 05, joining the pieces at the gold rings, where a seam hides best. Benny swims through all of it with the follow-camera at normal speed.

**What I can't promise:**
- **Repeats:** the same section repeats, so the paddles and the river pattern come back each ring (about once a second).
- **Seams:** a seam may show at a ring if the river doesn't line up perfectly.
- **Softness:** it stays a little softer than your flat painting. It won't get blurrier further in, though, because we always see the sharp part.

Starting the free test now: finding where the gold rings sit in the 3D world.

> # Creating worlds with multiple images with direction control
> In the  2D input mode of omnibox, drag in or upload up to 4 images.
> Click on the text overlay on image thumbnails to change its direction, and choose one from "Front", "Back", "Left", "Right".
> For this mode, images without overlap allow the marble models to creatively fill in the spaces between views.
> When you are done specifying the direction of each image, click on  Create to generate the world.
> For image file specifications, see [Prompt guidelines →](https://docs.worldlabs.ai/marble/create/prompt-guides.md)
> * Direction control is great for connecting different environments creatively.
> # Creating worlds with multiple images with Auto Layout
> In the  2D input mode of omnibox, drag in or upload up to 8 images.
> Toggle "Auto Layout" switch to on so the world model automatically determines the relative positioning of these images.

> ### How does API billing work?
> Billing for the World API is separate from billing for the Marble web app.
> * Credits purchased for the Marble app cannot be used with the API
> * API usage requires credits purchased through the World Labs Platform
> If you plan to use the API, make sure you purchase credits on the World Labs Platform, NOT in the Marble app.
> A Marble subscription — including a Free plan — says nothing about your API billing. The two are separate accounts even when you sign in with the same email, so being on the Marble Free plan does not mean your API usage is free.
> ### Why was I charged when I have not used the API recently?
> Almost certainly this is an overage charge, and it covers an earlier billing period.
> API credits are prepaid: you buy them up front and requests draw down that balance, so normal usage produces no charge at all. But requests are admitted based on a low-balance threshold rather than a per-request cost estimate, so a request that starts while your balance is low c …[+3247 chars]

### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T10:10:22.522Z

I've checked all four. I can't run Expand, Compose or HY-World 2.0 from here. The one option your existing credits can pay for is Marble 1.1 Plus.

| Tool | Can I run it? | Do your 3,610 credits pay? | Extends your existing world? | Chance of keeping the look |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Marble Expand** | No. It's a button in the Marble web app, not in the API this project uses. | No. The web app is billed separately from your API credits. | Yes, once: World Labs allows only one expansion per world. | Good near the join, by World Labs' own claim. But your world was made through the API, which World Labs runs as a separate account, so it may not appear in your web app at all. |
| **Marble 1.1 Plus** | Yes. | Yes: 1,580–3,080 credits, decided by World Labs during the job. Even the maximum leaves 530 credits, so no card charge is possible. | No. It makes a new world from the same clean approved picture, and a Plus world can't be expanded afterwards. | Best of these. It's the same model family and the same input that produced your good world. Plus is World Labs' "most expansive" option, meant for larger worlds. |
| **Marble Compose** | No (web app only). | No (web app billing). | It joins separate worlds. | Weakest. It means seams wherever two pieces meet. |

### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T10:38:49.507Z

No, I'm not saying that. You can have a real game where Benny swims through all of Screen 05 and it looks very close to your approved art. What nobody can give you is your painting's exact pixels in every frame.

**Marble 1.1 Plus result:** a small gain only. It holds up for about 3–3.5 seconds of swimming instead of 2.4, then the river smears the same way. It cost 1,580 of your existing credits, so your balance is 2,030. No cash was spent.

**Best route I've found for the whole level: the repeating tunnel.** It cuts one section between two gold rings from the sharp part of your current Marble world and joins copies of it end to end. Your painting is itself a tunnel of identical repeating rings, so this matches your art. In the test, Benny swam 10 units (about 7 seconds) and the river, rings and walls stayed as sharp at the end as at the start, with no blur creeping in.
- **Black far end:** the end of the tunnel shows a black hole, because I only drew three pieces ahead. When I tried drawing more, the test machine couldn't load them all. A lighter version of the distant pieces should fix that.
- **Repeats:** the river pattern repeats at every ring.

**SpAItial Echo-2: it isn't installed in this session.** It's listed in Anthropic's plugin directory as "spaitial-ai", and I've put the install card above.
- **Your own key needed:** you bring your own SpAItial API key. Whether it comes with free credits depends on your SpAItial account; I can't see that.
- **To run it:** install the card, add your key, then tell me. If its tools don't show up here, a new session may be needed.
- **The test:** I'll build one world from your clean Screen 05, test forward camera movement first, then Benny with the follow-camera. You'll get the beginning, middle and end at normal speed, compared directly to Marble's 2.4 seconds. I'll say plainly whether it beats it. No cash.

Everything is saved on GitHub (commit `e9c5889`). Your approved Screen 05, both Marble worlds and every gameplay version are unchanged.

> 
> ### Authentication
> 
> The SpAItial Developer API uses bearer tokens. Every request must include an `Authorization` header carrying a key issued to your account.
> 
> Your API key is a secret
> 
> Keys are shown to you exactly once at creation. Store them in a secret manager, never commit them, and never ship them inside a client-side bundle.
> 
> #### Obtaining a key[​](#obtaining-a-key "Direct link to Obtaining a key")
> 
> 1. Create a key in the dashboard
> 
>    Keys are created from the developer portal in the SpAItial dashboard. Each key belongs to your account and spends from the same shared credit balance used by the app.
> 
> 2. Pick the right scopes
> 
>    Each key has an explicit scope set (for example `worlds:create`, `worlds:read`, `worlds:write`, `files:create`). The scope is enforced server-side; calls outside the granted scopes return `403 forbidden`. Panorama edits use `worlds:create`; listing, reading, or downloading edited panoramas uses `worlds:read`.
> 
> 3. Check your credits
> 
>    World generation spends from your shared app and API credit balance. See [Credits and Billing](https://docs.spaitial.ai/api/credits-billing.md) for plan credits, purchases, and what API actions cost. …[+2268 chars]

> 
> Send a `multipart/form-data` request with a single `file` field:
> 
> ```bash
> API_KEY="[REDACTED]"
> 
> curl -sX POST "https://api.spaitial.ai/v1/files" \
>   -H "Authorization: Bearer $API_KEY" \
>   -F "file=@photo.jpg"
> 
> ```
> 
> Successful uploads return `201 Created`:
> 
> ```json
> {
>   "file_id": "file_abc123",
>   "content_type": "image/jpeg",
>   "file_size": 1843921,
>   "expires_at": "2026-05-21T10:42:00.000Z"
> }
> 
> ```
> 
> Allowed file types are JPEG, PNG, WebP, and GIF. The maximum file size is 25 MB. The API checks the detected MIME type from the file bytes, not just the uploaded filename or form content type.
> 
> #### Use the file in a world request[​](#use-the-file-in-a-world-request "Direct link to Use the file in a world request")
> 
> Pass the `file_id` as the world input:
>

### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T11:21:56.670Z

Echo-2 is not an improvement on Marble. From your picture's camera it matches your approved Screen 05 better than Marble did. Once the camera moves forward, though, it stays sharp only about as long as Marble's 2.4 seconds, then the river smears, and further on the world breaks up. I didn't run the Benny follow-camera test on it, because you asked for that only if the forward movement held up for longer. It used 160 of your free SpAItial credits and no cash.

**Your question: can Benny swim through it normally, like a real game, with your approved screen?**

**In any single AI world, no, not for a whole level.** Marble, Marble Plus and Echo-2 all hit the same limit. Each builds the world from your one picture, so you get about 2–3 seconds of real swimming before the river smears. That's a limit of building from one picture, not of any one tool.

**For a whole level, there is a way.** I tested it earlier today: the repeating tunnel, which joins copies of the sharpest stretch of your Marble world end to end. Your painting is itself a tunnel of identical gold rings, so this stays true to it. In the test Benny swam about 7 seconds and the scenery stayed as sharp at the end as at the start, with no smearing. It still needs fixing before it plays like a real game:
- **Black far end:** the tunnel end showed a black hole.
- **Repeating river:** the river pattern repeats at every ring.
- **Not in the game yet:** it then has to go into the actual game for a phone test.

All of that is free, no credits.

Everything is saved on GitHub (commit `475e16a`): your approved Screen 05, all the worlds (Marble ×3, Echo-2) and every gameplay version. Your remaining credits: Marble 2,030, SpAItial 640 free this month.

Should I finish the repeating tunnel and send you the full Screen 05 gameplay video?

### 🧑 User — 2026-11-21T11:21:56.841Z

CLAUDIA — MY FINAL GAMEPLAY AND VISUAL ACCEPTANCE REQUIREMENTS

I will be thrilled if my finished Screen 05 looks approximately 94–95% like my original approved artwork.

I am NOT demanding a perfect 100% pixel-for-pixel match.

But I AM demanding a real, playable 3D Android swimming game.

Think Subway Surfers, but instead of running, Benny swims through my beautiful approved 3D world.

Benny must paddle, move forward, steer left and right, and lean naturally as the river curves.

The camera must follow him. The gold rings, tunnel, machinery and scenery must actually pass around him as he moves forward.

I want the beautiful approved Screen 05 appearance maintained throughout the ENTIRE level, not only at the starting camera angle.

The gameplay should look clean, sharp, natural and polished. No noticeable blurring, smearing, disappearing scenery, glitches, repeated sections or unnatural camera movement.

Continue evaluating SpAItial Echo-2, but judge it by REAL forward-moving gameplay, not just a beautiful starting screenshot or a similarity score.

Show me one uninterrupted, normal-speed recording from the beginning of Screen 05 into Screen 06, with Benny swimming naturally the entire time.

If we can reach roughly 94–95% visual likeness during actual gameplay with smooth movement and no obvious visual defects, I will be happy to approve it.

Please preserve my original artwork and all successful game versions.

### 🤖 Claude — 2026-11-21T11:22:32.448Z

I can't promise 94–95% likeness across the whole level in real forward gameplay with nothing repeated. Here's why, and the one way I think gets close.

**Echo-2, judged by real forward movement:** the same limit as Marble. It's sharp for the first 2–3 seconds of swimming, then the river smears. On its own, it can't carry a whole level. That's true of every tool we've tried (Marble, Marble Plus, Echo-2), because each one builds the world from your single picture.

**How Subway Surfers actually does it:** the level is built from a handful of track pieces that keep coming back in different orders. Players don't notice, because there's variety and you're moving fast. That's the only way I can see to keep your Screen 05 sharp for the entire level:
- Cut the sharp first stretch, ring to ring, out of the worlds you already have: Echo-2 and Marble, plus mirrored versions. That gives about 4 different pieces, so no piece follows itself.
- Benny swims through them with the follow-camera, paddling, steering and leaning, at normal speed, from the start of Screen 05 into Screen 06.
- For a real 3D handoff, Screen 06 needs its own Echo-2 world from your approved 06 picture. That's 160 of your free SpAItial credits, no cash.

**What I can't promise:**
- **Likeness:** 94–95% isn't something I can promise. My honest expectation is that it clearly looks like your tunnel and colours, slightly softer and less glowy than your painting.
- **Repeats:** pieces will come back every few rings, just in varied order.
- **Joins:** where pieces from different worlds meet, a colour or shape difference may show. I'll match the colours as closely as I can.
- **Render time:** this machine renders the 3D world slowly, so the full Screen 05 → 06 recording will take several hours to produce.

Your original artwork and every saved version stay untouched either way.

Should I build it, including the Screen 06 Echo-2 world (160 free credits)?

**The three tools you named:**
- **Spark** is the 3D renderer I'm already using for these worlds. Its guidance for Android phones is 1–2 million splats, and the Screen 05 track uses about 800,000 at a time.
- **SplatTransform** is a free PlayCanvas command-line tool (version 3.12). It can remove stray floating bits, merge sections, and compress worlds into a much smaller format for phones. I can run it here.
- **SuperSplat** is PlayCanvas' editor that runs in a web browser, where you clean up a world by hand. I can't operate its interface from this session. SplatTransform covers the parts that can be automated, and you could do manual touch-ups in SuperSplat yourself.

**The other screens (04, 06, 07, 08, 09, 10):** each needs its own 3D world built from its own approved picture, so nothing from Screen 05 gets copied. That costs credits:

CLAUDIA — SCREEN 05: NATURAL 3D SWIMMING AND SWIPE CONTROLS

For Level 5, Screen 05 — Flow Into the Current, I want Benny, my pink character, to move naturally through the entire 3D environment, just like a character in a polished mobile game.

Movement requirements:

1. Swipe left: Benny smoothly swims toward the left side of the river.
2. Swipe right: Benny smoothly swims toward the right side.
3. Swipe up: Benny swims upward where the environment allows vertical movement.
4. Swipe down: Benny swims downward where the environment allows it.
5. Forward movement: Benny continuously swims forward through the river and golden tunnel rings.
6. Natural animation: His body, arms, and feet should move naturally while swimming. He should gently lean when turning.
7. Smooth camera: The camera must follow Benny naturally without shaking, sudden jumps, awkward angles, or clipping through objects.
8. Clear graphics: No blurriness, black patches, stretched textures, flickering, broken geometry, or anything visually unnatural.
9. Real responsiveness: Swipes must control Benny's actual 3D position, not just play a prerecorded animation or move the background.
10. Original appearance: Preserve the approved Screen 05's river, tunnel, colors, coins, scenery, and Benny's exact character design.

Make sure Benny stays properly positioned within the river, reacts smoothly to the player's controls, and can collect coins while moving.

Most importantly: I want players to feel like they're actually controlling Benny as he swims through this beautiful environment—not watching a video.

Please test the real gameplay with repeated left, right, up, and down swipes on an Android portrait screen, and show me a continuous gameplay recording demonstrating that everything works naturally.

My goal is smooth, responsive, visually clean gameplay while maintaining approximately 90–95% likeness to my original approved Screen 05.

CLAUDIA — SCREEN 05: STRICT GAMEPLAY REQUIREMENTS — DO NOT SHOW ME AN UNFINISHED OR BROKEN RESULT

Claudia, I want to be extremely clear about what I'm asking for with Level 5, Screen 05.

I want Benny, my original pink character, to swim through the entire 3D level naturally, just like a real, professionally developed mobile game.
